DEWSat on the Jetson Orin Nano: tables for the paper

Select a table, copy it, and paste it into Google Docs or Word. The same numbers are in the CSV files next to this page; README.md explains every column.

The manuscript's power, heat and speed statements against the board

J7_paper_claims_check.csv

Statement in the manuscriptWhat was measuredNumbersVerdictLimits of the test
ML processors stay below 70 CHottest on-chip sensor (CPU, GPU, SoC, junction) in every soak and benchmarkfirst run (worstcase_load_MAXN_SUPER_dvfs) (MAXN_SUPER, dynamic (DVFS), 60 min): 61.8 C; F10 flight15w (15W, dynamic (DVFS), 120 min): 47.0 C; F11 duty15w (15W, dynamic (DVFS), 60 min): 50.5 C; F12 worstcase (MAXN_SUPER, pinned (jetson_clocks), 60 min): 67.2 C; hottest in any benchmark row 66.7 C (MAXN_SUPER, trtexec encoder_fp16_dyn b100 cudagraph)Met: highest 67.2 C, 2.8 C below the board's 70 C passive tripRoom air with the stock fan, on the developer kit's carrier board. Vacuum (no convection) was not tested; in orbit the heat must leave by conduction, so this does not show the flight unit stays below 70 C.
Onboard compute fits 10-25 WModule input power (VDD_IN, the module's own INA3221 sensor): idle, running the paper model on tiles, and the highest draw seenIdle 4.40-6.94 W across modes and clock settings (at rest where measured); paper model on 100-window tiles 7W DVFS 6.38 W, 7W pinned 6.39 W, 15W DVFS 8.57 W, 15W pinned 9.27 W, 25W pinned 12.92 W, MAXN_SUPER DVFS 14.08 W, MAXN_SUPER pinned 14.12 W; highest mean over any benchmark row 20.50 W (MAXN_SUPER, trtexec fused10_fp32_dyn b4096); highest sustained (soak) 17.52 W; the module's over-current protection fired 18 times in F12 worstcase (MAXN_SUPER, pinned (jetson_clocks), 60 min; 0 errors); 13,900 times in the Prithvi benchmark MAXN_SUPER_dvfs; it fired in none of the other 12 runsWithin 25 W in every mode tested; the paper model on tiles drew 1.93-9.62 W above the idle for the budgetModule power only: the carrier board, SSD, fan, radio, camera and power conversion are not included. The 10-25 W envelope was written for the 12U design; the 6U budget needs its own number.
Inference under 10 ms per tile (NPU target)Paper model, one tile = 100 windows, TensorRT FP16 engine, trtexec latency including copies7W DVFS 4.103 ms (p99 4.119), 7W pinned 4.101 ms (p99 4.114), 15W DVFS 1.831 ms (p99 1.854), 15W pinned 1.792 ms (p99 1.803), 25W pinned 1.223 ms (p99 1.232), MAXN_SUPER DVFS 1.129 ms (p99 1.146), MAXN_SUPER pinned 1.127 ms (p99 1.136)Met in every mode: slowest 4.103 ms p50 (7W, DVFS)The Orin Nano has no DLA (NVIDIA's NPU-style engine); these ran on its GPU. A tile here is about 100 cells' 12-month windows, the kit's definition; the image preprocessing that makes the windows is not timed.
Accelerators are duty-cycledF11: 60 s of tiles, 240 s idle, for 60 min at 15W; and the time to boot from a rebootOn 7.74 W, off 4.50 W, average 5.15 W over the test; reboot command to SSH 50-56 s over 6 reboots, systemd boot 16.0-18.5 sMeasured: idle between bursts costs the idle power above; powering the board off between passes trades that for about a minute of bootOff-phase power is idle module power with the model loaded, not power-gated; the board was never switched off, so off-state power was not measured.
One copy or the ten-copy average (left open in the ML draft)Same tile (100 windows), TensorRT, trtexec: the ten-seed ensemble (fused, the paper model) vs one seed7W DVFS: ten 4.103 ms / 26.0 mJ, one 1.047 ms / 5.75 mJ; 7W pinned: ten 4.101 ms / 26.1 mJ, one 1.043 ms / 5.67 mJ; 15W DVFS: ten 1.831 ms / 15.4 mJ, one 0.656 ms / 4.13 mJ; 15W pinned: ten 1.792 ms / 16.4 mJ, one 0.656 ms / 4.39 mJ; 25W pinned: ten 1.223 ms / 15.6 mJ, one 0.442 ms / 3.88 mJ; MAXN_SUPER DVFS: ten 1.129 ms / 15.6 mJ, one 0.446 ms / 4.16 mJ; MAXN_SUPER pinned: ten 1.127 ms / 15.7 mJ, one 0.407 ms / 3.85 mJTen copies took 2.5-3.9x the time and 3.7-4.6x the energy of one copy per tileOnly the ten-seed ensemble's accuracy is reported (AUC 0.663); a single seed's accuracy was not computed. The one-seed engine ran in FP32 (TensorRT chose no FP16 layers) while the ten-copy engine ran in FP16.
(Prithvi fresh-test model, for comparison) the same per-tile targetOne region-month: the encoder on one region pass (100 chips = 25 cells x 4 chips) plus the GRU head on the region's 25 windows; CPU (ONNX Runtime FP32) and TensorRT FP167W DVFS: CPU not possible (ONNX Runtime aborts in 7W), TensorRT FP16 286.2 ms (outputs did NOT match); 15W DVFS: CPU 2.89 s (outputs match), TensorRT FP16 118.7 ms (outputs did NOT match); MAXN_SUPER DVFS: CPU 2.56 s (outputs match), TensorRT FP16 80.1 ms (outputs did NOT match)Only the CPU path gave the reference outputs, at 2.56-2.89 s per region-month; TensorRT FP16 timed 24-32x faster in the same modes, but its encoder engines gave wrong outputs on this boardNot the paper's model. Its units differ: a cell needs 4 image chips per satellite pass, and the encoder runs once per pass, the head once per month. On this board TensorRT 10.3 did not reproduce the encoder (any batch, FP32 or FP16) or the GRU head's variable-batch engines (P2); ONNX Runtime aborts in the 7W mode.
